# LLP 3000.000.000.002: Declarative release backfills

**Type:** RFC
**Status:** Review
**Systems:** Snapback 4 language, storage, release adoption, staging clock, proof kit
**Author:** Codex for Charlie Cheever (MIG)
**Date:** 2026-10-19
**Revised:** 2026-10-19
**Related:** [Release activation](./3000.000.000-retained-storage-and-hot-release.rfc.md), [Staging](./3000.000.000.001-staging-with-real-users.rfc.md)

## 1. Commission

Implementer: MIG, 2026-10-19. Wave 17's fifty changes require constant,
conditional, derived, enum, string-split and point-lookup transformations.
Declare these in Quarry, with no public migration mutation, job or JavaScript.
REL5 owns release selection/restoration and old-client compatibility; SEC3 owns
security checks. No new version, publication or push authority is implied.

## 2. Language

A top-level declaration describes one table's historical input fields and its
simultaneous output patch:

```quarry
backfill bookings vehicleClass(weight: int, length: int):
  { vehicleClass: 'commercial' if weight > 3500 else 'van' if length >= 500 else 'car' }
```

Here length is stored in centimetres. Arguments are typed historical fields;
they remain in the declaration after the candidate drops those fields. The
compiler binds arguments against their explicit types, not today's table.
The runtime validates them against each interpreted historical row. Output
names belong to candidate authoritative columns and may equal input names:
read-old/write-new. Outputs of multiple declarations on a table must be
disjoint. Names are unique per table. Durable store history keeps one
normalized definition per table/name, including gaps; changing it refuses with a
bridge/new-name repair. A new name denotes a new transformation.

The expression body is an object patch. It uses Quarry's existing arithmetic,
comparisons, null handling, conditional expressions, immutable helper expansion,
and bounded deterministic scalar builtins. Supported text additions are
`before(text, delimiter)`, `after`, `beforeLast`, `afterLast`, `startsWith`,
and `allDigits`. Splits remove the first/last exact delimiter; absent delimiter
means whole text before, empty text after. An empty split delimiter refuses.
Digits means nonempty ASCII digits. Text is Unicode, comparisons and splits
are locale independent. Existing `lower`, `trim`, `slice`, `count`, `sha256`,
`first`, `last`, `at`, `min`, `max`, exact `divide`, calendar/civil transforms
and concatenation retain their normal checked semantics. No randomness,
viewer, clock, minted ids, writes, mutation helpers or loops are admitted.
Identity `id` is never an output. Only scalar outputs are admitted, including new references. Assets, lists,
engine-owned/captured/maintained and lifetime outputs refuse, including fields
owned by a historical source lifetime even when the candidate removes that rule.
Restored narrowed rows retain their individual output ownership, captures and
reference types. Columns added by later predecessor schemas contribute their
own types/defaults only when absent from that archived definition. Overwriting an
existing reference/asset is refused: derive a new reference column so the
existing dormant reference-lifetime ledger owns retirement/restoration.

Point reads use the ordinary `members[principal = owner] ! else NOT_FOUND`
spelling. They require a unique point. A missing asserted point refuses;
ordinary nullable point reads and `exists` retain their explicit semantics.
Historical existence predicates may traverse a metered source range, needed
for wave-17 cross-tower references; they return no row collection. A meter
cut is a refusal, never false absence or an incomplete historical value.
Before evaluating any patch, each unique lookup index validates the historical
population's cardinality with bounded privileged duplicate samples. A different
simultaneous patch making candidate keys unique cannot choose an arbitrary old
match. All reads run as the trusted activation system principal, bypassing application
read rules, against the assembled historical snapshot. Dormant namespaces
remain unnameable. Candidate read indexes are built over that source snapshot
only when their component fields exist there. No JavaScript or app migration
operation receives this authority. All declarations are self-contained: they
cannot read another declaration's outputs in the same activation.

## 3. Adoption and reversibility

The existing activation writer transaction owns this sequence:

1. Assemble retained source rows/cells and their source definitions using the
   existing restoration intent. This includes narrowed rows eligible to return:
   restore their full historical images before evaluation, deferring only the
   backfill output columns' candidate-type checks. Retained reference invalidation,
   source lifetime and audience checks still apply before restoration. Retire
   source-expired lifetimes before any backfill; a transform cannot extend an
   expiry or opening boundary.
2. When leaving a declaration, restore its predecessor-visible output fields
   from saved **output preimages**, separately from its argument inputs. Save
   interpreted old values, including literal defaults/nulls, and absence.
   Added output columns use ordinary dormant retention instead. A different
   current value from both recorded output and preimage refuses with a conflict;
   untouched same-column enum renames restore their exact original value.
   Membership is per row lifetime, with normalized declaration definitions kept
   beside evidence. Removing a whole table suspends its membership rather than
   feeding transformed values back as inputs. Re-entering that declaration reads
   its original overwritten inputs and preserves its current result; selecting
   original source instead reverses it even when the immediately previous schema
   lacked the table/declaration. Ordinary active deletion clears membership.
3. Enter declarations absent from the immediately active schema. Unchanged
   carry-forward and reopening run none for ordinary active rows: candidate-native
   writes are not historical rows. Newly restored narrowed rows receive carried
   declarations too, including a declaration introduced while they were dormant. On re-entry, restored cells/rows are eligible along with
   older active rows. A native v2 row whose absent historical inputs have a
   complete valid restored output image keeps that image. Older v1 rows lacking
   outputs are filled. Declarations must accompany skipped-release source;
   no unseen intermediate transformation is implicitly inferred.
4. Evaluate every entering patch against one assembled source snapshot, with
   ordinary per-row job meters and a paged source walk. Stage output/evidence
   in SQLite temp storage before applying any patch. Recompute the patch on
   re-entry, so changed lookup values count as changed dependencies even when
   declared arguments are unchanged. Compare both saved argument values and
   computed outputs. If unchanged, preserve edited added-column cells. If
   changed, recompute only when current outputs equal the saved result or the
   reversed preimage; otherwise refuse the conflict. Never overwrite a manual
   output edit because another input happened to change. A fully reversed
   same-column output edited during absence also refuses re-entry when its
   current interpreted value equals neither preimage nor saved result, even
   with unchanged arguments. Sparse defaults count by interpreted value.
5. Apply patches and evidence together. Run ordinary narrowing/reference
   closure, projection, complete type/asset validation, indexes, uniqueness,
   caps and maintained-data rebuilds. Transformed dependencies force rebuilds
   even when their declarations did not change. Publish programs, generation
   and release in the same commit. Any error rolls everything back.

Evidence is keyed by table number, row lifetime and the SHA-256 digest of the
normalized declaration/typed IR (no diagnostic locations or whitespace).
It records declared input values, each overwritten output preimage and computed
output. Ordinary authoritative deletion/expiry deletes that row's evidence in
its transaction; id reuse cannot inherit it. Row moves to dormant storage
preserve lifetime. Backfill definitions enter schema/activation identity;
changed rows also set activation population, forcing a fresh generation and
device reacquisition. Public/device schema projections omit all backfill
metadata; devices never execute migrations. A successful evidence installation
sets `snapback4-release-store-5`, transactionally. Unaware binaries refuse that
format even when an old source is selected. Failed installation retains the
previous format. Legacy stores without backfills remain unchanged. Prune previews
backfill preimages separately, with counts, bytes and bounded ids. Explicit
prune deletes their values and keeps only per-lifetime reversal-unavailable
identities. Leaving an overwritten column then refuses with a pruned-preimage
reason: select a bridge retaining present meaning or restore a backup. Active
row deletion clears these identities too. Pruning added-column evidence does
not prevent rollback when no old-visible field needs a preimage.

Dropping a declaration has explicit semantic meaning: leave it by restoring
preimages or refusing conflicts. Keep declarations in every later source
whose view should retain their meaning. Refusals include exact affected counts
and up to five bounded privileged samples. Missing inputs/lookups, output/type
errors, arithmetic/meter errors and uniqueness/cap conflicts refuse atomically;
a widened/optional non-unique bridge plus an authorized repair mutation is the
repair path. A post-backfill-edit conflict requires keeping the current/bridge
source, deciding a truthful old representation, then repairing it before
selecting old source. No automatic fallback turns a new enum value into an old
meaning. Native v2 rows with no preimage retain the parent's existing law:
forward selection may make incompatible present values dormant; rollback
requires representability and refuses with its existing reason. This amendment
does not change REL5's rollback or restoration selection policy.

`deploy --check` uses the existing private disk snapshot, deploy direction and
restoration intent; rollback previews use rollback direction. Later live writes
can change its plan. `backfills` in both cold/hot JSON receipts and exact preview
text reports `table name transformed N rows` or `table name reversed on N rows`.
These are transformed row visits, separate from the parent's disjoint dormant
rows/cells/index entries. They are not additive counts of distinct rows across
different transformations, and do not claim a frozen production population.

Consumed historical references must retain valid parent-lifetime evidence even
when the input column is dropped. Expired predecessor/restored images are retired
before any transform, including ordinary expiry without read-through. Pruning
keeps membership-only tombstones solely for surviving lifetimes; such a tombstone
refuses re-entry requiring lost values rather than treating the row as fresh.

Re-entry retains an adoption-cohort identity with each active row receipt.
All declarations first applied in one activation, including continuously carried
declarations on tables that were never removed, read one reconstructed source
snapshot, including point/range indexes; this overlays that cohort's complete
input evidence and output preimages (including original absence) on current
images. Distinct original cohorts are evaluated separately against their
respective historical overlays, so a declaration introduced later can read an
earlier declaration's already-transformed values. Computed and retained/native output values must fit candidate scalar types and
text minimum/format constraints before narrowing; invalid generated outputs
refuse the entire adoption with affected-row samples. Patches remain staged until
all cohorts succeed, and current row images are restored before applying them.
New declarations use the current assembled predecessor snapshot. Inactive
cohorts restore their own still-omitted input interpretations, independently of
new declarations in that activation, and catch up changed authored inputs. Declared historical arguments consume retained
cells and source definitions even when the candidate omits those columns;
retained parent-lifetime bindings still apply. A native-image exception requires a birth marker for this exact declaration,
a matching fingerprint of the original non-output inputs, and valid, complete outputs;
a new declaration never skips valid old inputs merely because an older
transformation left a retained output. Receipt reference inputs use private namespaced bindings in the existing
dormant parent-lifetime ledger, independently of the live input column. A
retargeted/dropped input cannot substitute its current binding for the saved
reference. Every unchanged saved argument consumed during active or inactive
re-entry validates its private lifetime binding before program evaluation.
Bindings use each restored narrowed row's actual archived reference type
(rather than the table-wide predecessor), even when an
argument is declared as generic `id`. Existing private bindings survive
re-entry with unchanged saved inputs even after the visible column becomes
generic `id`; schema type erasure cannot recapture the same parent id. Reconstruction validates any consumed saved binding before restoring
its value. Original absence is recorded separately from the predecessor's interpreted
value. A physically missing field consumes its source default when present,
otherwise null; receipts retain that interpretation explicitly, preventing
later defaults from changing argument or lookup/index evaluation. Expired sources retire when entering or leaving the
last declaration, including restored table images.

When transformed rows become dormant, candidate output reference types supply
parent bindings even when the predecessor had no such column. Existing invalid
bindings are preserved. If a restored row becomes dormant again, only its
explicitly backfilled authoritative cells update the retained image; original
source definition, other cells and authority provenance stay intact. Forward
selection after rollback reconstructs absent added outputs for that same
declaration before evaluating its arguments, rather than feeding it its own
retained result.

Input omission is stored independently from interpreted null/default values,
both for the current physical lifetime and for each receipt. Ordinary writes
compare interpreted old/new inputs; meaningful edits end omission provenance,
while unrelated writes and default materialization do not. Receipts reconstruct
their original interpretation of omitted fields across later default changes.

Rows inserted while a declaration is active receive a private native-birth
marker keyed by its normalized definition and a hash of their input image;
no original input values are kept in that marker. The same declaration preserves
complete native images with unchanged inputs, including absent optional inputs,
constant transforms and whole-table restoration. Changed native inputs, invalid
present inputs, lost native output images or conflicting other-transform history
refuse with a bridge reason. An inactive overlapping receipt is compatible only
when every overlapping current output equals its recorded preimage; an active
or pruned conflicting history still refuses. Same-column output values belong
to the candidate type, rather than the historical input type, for native rows.
A new declaration name does not inherit another
name's birth marker. Prune retains only native fingerprints for surviving
lifetimes, alongside current omission flags; receipt absence flags are forgotten
with historical values. Delete/reset remove every marker for the ended lifetime.

Source retirement uses the ordinary engine expiry/reference-action closure
under the complete assembled predecessor schema, before any patch runs or
removed table is archived. Only surviving post-action rows enter dormant storage. Restored source
indexes and cap occupancy are rebuilt first; writing-reference groups must fit
the source reservation's live bound. Cascade and set-null effects participate
in this same transaction; restrict or retained-child blockers refuse the entire
adoption. Retired source population forces a new generation so clients cannot
keep children removed by a cascade. Authoritative set-null updates corresponding archived cells/narrowed images
to null before clearing old column bindings, while receipt input bindings remain
independent. An expired
narrowed source is exposed only inside this uncommitted lifecycle phase and
cannot reach a backfill evaluation alive.

Development reset still empties every lifetime's data/history/native/omission
markers, then reconstructs code-derived declaration/input registries from its
retained schema before reseeding; the new identity's rows receive new birth
markers. No release HTTP reset door is added.

Evidence decode is preflighted against
the existing job heap ceiling. Text preview lists private preimages separately
from dormant application rows/cells/index entries.

### Per-column transformed views

A receipt records a `retained_outputs` set independently of its declaration's
active flag. When leaving a declaration, candidate-visible transformed outputs
reverse to their preimages. Outputs absent from the candidate remain transformed
in dormant storage; their set membership survives until reversal or re-entry.
Even an inactive declaration reverses those suspended fields when a later plain
schema exposes them. Whole-table suspension keeps the transformed view intact.

Re-entry preserves the saved preimage for each still-transformed output. A
fully reversed output instead captures its actual assembled source value,
including restored dormant cells and interpreted predecessor defaults, before
applying the patch. Input history and rollback preimages are separate: an input
may retain its original omitted/null interpretation while the latest rollback
view has a literal default. Ordinary writes do not erase omission provenance
when they edit a transformed output from that same original cohort.

An inverse restoring genuine absence records a transaction-local source mask.
Later evaluation cannot fill that absence from the suspended result or a default
belonging to the output schema. The mask is dropped on every exit. Earlier
recorded cohorts' staged patches are replayed into later recorded source views;
fresh declarations still share the untouched predecessor snapshot and cannot
read each other's newly staged outputs. Every decode remains heap-admitted.

Reference arguments copy any matching ordinary cell-lifetime bindings before
restoration consumes that ledger, including transformed reference cells absent
from an archived whole-row definition. Unchanged saved inputs retain existing
private bindings; inferred current bindings cannot overwrite their invalidation.
The stable format is `snapback4-release-store-5`, with schema capability
`release-backfills-v2`. Published 0.4.33's store format 3 upgrades on first use.
Unpublished experimental format 4 and the older capability cannot silently read
this evidence layout. Package versions are not changed in this workstream.

## 4. Staging clock

Only a store durably classified staging by deploy --staging may serve with
--clock-offset. Missing/unknown classification is production. This setting
is process-local, with the same signed-duration parser and monotone elapsed
clock as dev. No serve-time mechanism can reclassify an existing production store. Restart drops the offset unless explicitly
provided again; persisted timed-disclosure floors never retreat below their captured value.

Staging exposes the existing /dev/clock/status and /dev/clock/advance only
with any full service session: these are trusted operator credentials, as for
system jobs. Production refuses even with a service token, and an explicit
production serve offset refuses with E_CLOCK_DEV_ONLY before listener startup. Account, guest, read-only and unauthenticated
requests refuse. Recheck classification and session under the engine writer
before advancing. All fixture, reset, persona and dev-job doors remain closed.
Use the same advance implementation, schedule drain and ambiguous-reply law
as dev. CLI clock advance --release DIR mints its local service authority.
startServe accepts clockOffset and exposes now()/advance() through that
operator authority; it never changes store classification. Each helper control
mints a current staging-time operator session rather than caching a credential
across arbitrary time jumps. Ordinary user sessions retain normal expiry.

Hot release activation samples `shared.now()` under its engine writer, including
the persisted disclosure floor, and passes that one time through the core's
explicit `adopt_release_at`. A live preview probes the existing filesystem owner lock independently of
HTTP bind/address hints and acquires a stopped-store lock before taking its
SQLite snapshot, retaining that lock through inspection. A rollback preview
reuses its caller's existing ownership rather than probing its own lock as a
live host.
A live preview samples time through the same-owner,
store-bound existing private status protocol; no session is minted or clock
changed. A missing existing-store ownership sidecar refuses rather than assuming a
stopped host without exclusion; ordinary rollback preview can reestablish and
reuse its canonical ownership. A revision mismatch with the private database snapshot refuses as a
racing activation. Store contents and the clock are point-in-time samples, not
a freeze of later live writes. A stopped store's cold activation/preview uses
wall time, with the existing persisted disclosure floor applied by activation.
Positive and negative staging offsets are both covered by hot/preview tests.

## 5. Evidence

Tests cover all fifty historical shapes, no-op/restart, v1 writes during
rollback, same-column enum reversal, edited-output refusal, missing lookup,
invalid output, uniqueness failure, deletion/id reuse, generation reset and
hot/cold/preview equivalence. The guide's persistent production recipe uses
real users and operator role setup, tests tightened privacy and declares its
invariant, rolls back and forward with business assertions. Its exact fences
run against the fresh packed tarball. Reproduce five copied bakeoff lanes,
record actual line/time deltas. Run required crate/client/differential/Linux,
Mac Studio clock tests, refs, budget and root checks.

Crates: `snapback4-core` (value model, schema with `sync`, IR, key encoding,
SQLite store, rules with hoisting, the meter, the interpreter, the partition
log, at-most-once, the day-two classifier, wire forms), `snapback4-lang`
(Quarry lexer, parser, binder, diagnostics, the TypeScript emitter),
`snapback4` (verbs and the HTTP wire), `snapback4-sim` (the deterministic
world hosting the engine and a replica device), `snapback4-difftest` (seeded
Quarry source generator, rewrite and meter oracles, optional Postgres store
oracles with prefetch on/off, the server-against-device oracle on both device stores, the engine-against-Lean-model oracle, structural shrinking and replay; not published or linked by shipped crates), `snapback4-device` (the
interpreter over a `DeviceStore`: the core's SQLite under the native
module for Expo and Exact2 hosts, memory under wasm on the web; one
dispatcher, one suite on both)). The guide (`snapback4-lang/src/guide.rs`) is
the language card and the keyword cards; packages: `snapback4` (the client
over the device and the CLI shim), `snapback4-darwin-arm64` and `snapback4-linux-x64` (the binaries), `snapback4-expo`
(the Swift module over the device's C ABI).

What exists, by week (LLP 3000 §5):

| Week | Lands |
|---|---|
| 1 | the contract (`packages/snapback4/src/contract.ts`), the mocked chat app against it, the hub fixture wrapper, the five floor measurements in the journal, the simulator skeleton (`crates/snapback4-sim`), the Snapback 2 corpus copied in, 0.0.1 published |
| 2 | server: SQLite transaction and interpreter, rules and uniformity, the meter, the partition log (`sync`), generations, personas, the wire, `check`/`dev`/`run`/`test`/`why`/`guide`, the seven simulator invariants |
| 3 | web client: replica store, outbox with client-minted ids, local interpreter, reactive local queries, the online-only tier, the chat app on the real client |
| 4 | Expo client, day-two scenarios, deploy, the loop bar, the Clique hub run, the comparison record |

Gotchas you cannot derive from the code:

- Own Cargo and Bun workspaces; the repository root excludes them. Run cargo/bun from `snapback4/`. Node 24.x (`engines`): run the suite with the pinned runtime on PATH, not the ambient Homebrew node.
- Web builds require `bun install --frozen-lockfile` first. Binaryen 132.0.0 is
  an exact workspace devDependency; build/measurement invoke its portable
  `node_modules/binaryen/bin/wasm-opt` through Node, never a PATH optimizer.
  Missing/mismatched dependencies and optimizer failures refuse the build.
  See README's web-device build instructions and HV round 4 for host parity.
- The suite schedules client files in one pool: `max(1, min(24,
  floor(os.availableParallelism() / 3)))` slots (3 on 10 CPUs, 24 on 96).
  This counts Linux CPU affinity and leaves room for each file's servers,
  browser renderers and workers. Acquisition and server-e2e share that pool;
  there are no extra shard processes outside it. Real-timer files listed in
  `TIMER_CLIENT_FILES` drain that pool and run individually afterward with
  unchanged timing bounds. The quote-jump browser witness remains pooled.
  The two full-count model
  files retain a separate serial slot. `SNAPBACK4_TEST_SERIAL_CLIENT_FILES=1`
  still forces one client file at a time. The external file watchdog measures
  time since a subtest started or finished: max(900 seconds, largest single
  declared guard + max(60 seconds, 25%)). There is no absolute duration cap.
  Console output does not count as progress. Serial mode uses the same bounds.
  See `scripts/run-client-tests.mjs` and the QF round-2 guard census; update
  named work counts and recheck the census when declarations change. Every
  file runs as native `node --test` with default isolation, in a process group
  whose first member is a tiny Node keeper. The runner closes the keeper's stdin
  on completion, stall or abort; only the keeper calls `kill(0, SIGKILL)`.
  Runner death also closes that pipe. An unexpectedly exited keeper fails the
  file and never authorizes a runner signal. Cleanup requires two consecutive
  completed inspections (50 ms apart) with no living group member. Observed leftovers
  fail as TEST_FILE_LEAK. Failed listings and unclassifiable PID/PPID/PGID
  prefixes remain fail-closed. A ps timeout at two seconds retries once with a
  60-second bound; all other command errors refuse immediately. Listings use
  asynchronous spawn so other files' timers and keeper EOF delivery keep running.
  File watchdogs and cleanup's five-second window use per-file monotonic time.
  Expiry requires another completed snapshot: a live group after the deadline
  refuses; two consecutive empty snapshots pass even when the second is late.
  Precisely
  recognized exit-time routing anomalies
  discard the whole snapshot and retry at most twice, 50 ms apart; persistent
  anomalies refuse and never confer ownership (LLP 3000 §7, QF2).
  Metadata is validated for group members and
  their visible descendants; unrelated rows only increment diagnostics.
  Signed Darwin UIDs, variable lstart spacing and comm spaces are accepted.
  Vanished members count as exited. Processes leaving the group (setsid,
  setpgid or detached spawns) are outside cleanup custody; visible descendants
  receive metadata validation but are never signalled by the runner. Native Node's exit
  status alone decides test success; reporter counts are diagnostic only.
  The reporter observes progress as Node delivers it: synchronous work that
  does not yield may buffer events in Node's child until that work finishes.
  Such an unreported interval is subject to the same stall bound. See QF Round 7.
  The M5 MacBook is the publication host; the office M1 is calibration-only.
- Client tests and their helpers must gate Linux APIs (`/proc`, `strace`, `ss`,
  `taskset`, namespace/network tools) at the test or call, or implement a
  portable fallback. Keep portable assertions running on Darwin and give each
  skipped witness an explicit reason. The client pool runs
  `test-platform-audit.test.mjs`: a cheap static census follows static named/namespace `.mjs`
  imports and named helpers and checks scoped Linux skips/branches/early returns.
  A gate in another test does not count. This conservative common-case guard
  does not prove dynamic imports, generated scripts or arbitrary control flow;
  review those paths and run affected tests on the publication Mac.
  Listener snapshots and syscall tracing are Linux qualification capabilities,
  never dependencies of the published `snapback4/proof` browser helper.
- Before a publication dry run, warm the test artifacts **in that publication
  worktree**, while the host is free of calibration work:
  `LIBSQLITE3_FLAGS=-USQLITE_ENABLE_MEMORY_MANAGEMENT cargo test --workspace --no-run`.
  Use the dry run's toolchain, target directory, profile/features, `RUSTFLAGS`
  and Cargo profile environment. Do not add `--release` or `--profile production`:
  the suite uses the default test profile. The suite still asks Cargo for the
  complete test inventory, runs every binary and runs doctests separately.
  Warming compiles without executing tests and supplies no qualification receipt.
- One owner on the core (LLP 3000 decision 4): the engine, the store and the client contract are written by the lead session; Codex lanes take the fixture generator, the reference apps, the device witnesses and the periphery. Do not reshape `contract.ts` from a lane; propose the change in the journal.
- No ceiling is raised in slice one (LLP 2000.000 §12). A thing that does not fit changes the design or waits.
- The journal is append-only and dated; never rewrite an entry. LLP 3000 §7 carries decisions that must outlive it.
- Nothing here depends on `snapback2/` at runtime. Copy source deliberately, split under 1,500 lines on the way in, and say so in the journal.
- Named lane: `~/projects/snapback-sb4` on `sb4/*` branches. Never `git stash`; commit checkpoints.
- The conformance corpus (`corpus/`) is Snapback 2's serialized IR and value
  model; the core keeps those serializations so it binds. A case the slice
  cannot run is skipped with a named reason, never counted as a pass.
- `snapback/generated/api.ts` is built by `check` and `dev`, never committed;
  the app's typecheck needs a `check` first (the suite does one).
- Point rule probes and `exists` require a unique key. A fully pinned superset
  of a required unique key is unique too (for example an explicit role index
  containing the unique conversation/principal pair). Nullable unique key
  arguments need non-null proof. Other existence checks use bounded populations
  in code. Named equalities covering a total unique key check extra columns on
  that one row without an additional index.
- The packaged CLI uses `LIBSQLITE3_FLAGS=-USQLITE_ENABLE_MEMORY_MANAGEMENT` during both publication tests and its production build (LLP 3000 §7). A plain production Cargo build retains the dependency's ordinary SQLite policy; use the same build input when comparing with the package. The variable is unnecessary at runtime.
- When creating a stage, `scripts/publish.mjs` runs the suite and refuses on red, builds the production binary, packs `snapback4-darwin-arm64` and verifies the installed digest, then publishes both packages. Bump `version` in `packages/snapback4/package.json` (and its `optionalDependencies`), `packages/snapback4-darwin-arm64/package.json`,
  `packages/snapback4-linux-x64/package.json` and `Cargo.toml` together; a test refuses a mismatch.
- Publication retains exact qualified tarballs under `target/publication/`. If a
  stage exists, use `node scripts/publish.mjs --resume` to validate its recorded
  suite qualification, requalify those exact bytes through the installed-consumer
  proof, and finish missing registry artifacts; an existing version must match
  its staged integrity. This also applies after `--dry-run`. An ordinary invocation
  refuses an existing stage so changed source cannot silently publish old bytes.
  Bump the version to build a new pair; preserve a partially published stage.
- Cross-host publication (LLP 3000.021 §3.3): prepare the common client once
  from clean release source, qualify that tarball on Linux, and import the
  Linux tarball plus its receipt on the Mac. Source commit, client SRI and
  Linux SRI must agree. Linux prefers working Docker/Podman, otherwise uses checksum-pinned proot
  with clean Ubuntu 24.04/Alpine root filesystems in a user cache, and refuses
  `NPM_TOKEN` or `NODE_AUTH_TOKEN`, even when empty. It never signs or packs
  the client. The Mac never rebuilds or executes Linux; `--resume` rechecks
  its copied receipt. No receipt packs a Darwin-only client; an existing
  two-platform preparation is refused on that path. Packed dependencies must
  match the staged set. Uploads use verified private snapshots, with each
  digest checked again immediately before publish.
  Commands and current qualification limits:
  [`reports/20260927-lx-linux-package/REPORT.md`](./reports/20260927-lx-linux-package/REPORT.md).
- The guide is tested text. Every ```quarry block in `crates/snapback4-lang/src/guide.rs` and in `packages/snapback4/README.md` is compiled by `crates/snapback4-lang/tests/guide.rs` with no diagnostic and no note (the language card is also run against the engine); every refusal code the sources emit must be in `guide::CODES`; every `guide("..")` a diagnostic carries must name a card. Add a form to the language card when you add it to the parser, fence README Quarry as ```quarry, and never fence Quarry as plain text to dodge the test.
- Native jobs (LLP 3000.005) need `rustup target add wasm32-unknown-unknown`; the suite's fixture build fails loudly without it. The guest SDK `crates/snapback4-native` is a path dependency until it is published; an app's `snapback/native/Cargo.toml` names it by path.

Quarry store testing (LLP 3000.035 §5.1): `SNAPBACK4_EVAL_POSTGRES` is an
explicit evaluation connection. The factory owns one physical schema per worker,
restores its rows and bookkeeping, installs the compiled fixture, then opens the
ordinary engine. Mutation attempts restore a seeded snapshot; shrinking reuses
that same factory. `Database::connect_fresh()` is an opt-in test mode. Fresh and
restored outcomes must serialize to identical bytes. The local wire observer
retains SQLSTATE, message, detail and SQL statement before the public query API
converts a driver error to a refusal; it never changes the traffic.

Reads sweep every public boundary. Mutations compare every discovered position
twice on SQLite, and compare up to eight seeded positions per dimension on
Postgres (including first/last), plus the case's original budget. Sampling
counters are in `store_stats`; each selected position runs prefetch on and off.
Physical prefetch counters are separate from the exact public outcome record.
The widened generator (`src/widen.rs`) emits prefetch-reachable reads: pages
projecting `tags[.tag]` and `tags[name = .label]` (a `Get` and a unique
`Lookup` whose keys vary across rows) and a loop reading `tags[row.tag]`, so
`store_stats` reports marked reads, transferred rows, cases with a transfer
and `prefetch_consumption_witness_probes` (fewer database calls with prefetch
on: cached answers were consumed). A planted cached-hit-to-miss fault is caught
by the random corpus (LLP 3000.035 §5.1; the closed
[coverage ticket](../issues/closed/20261007-quarry-store-oracle-prefetch-coverage-gap.md)).
Heap is charged identically on every store and the device (LLP 3000.007
§20.24.5); any disagreement is a ticket candidate, never normalized into a pass.
The cluster regression asserts it finds no signature and refuses any new one.

`run-stores --seconds N` stops each worker between cases after N seconds; a case
and any shrinking already underway finish. `cases` remains the requested count,
`generated` is actual completed generation, and `time_limited_workers` reports
the early stop. Slowest-case timing includes generation, comparisons, shrinking
and artifact saving. It does not silently claim the requested million. The scheduled
runner rejects incomplete reports; its own `--hours` option instead chooses a
case count from a 256-case calibration of both campaigns, with 20% headroom and
a million-case cap. This is a sizing estimate, not a hard deadline.

Generator widening (`src/widen.rs`, LLP 3000.035 §5.1): from a stream
independent of the base generator (`generate::base`; a seed it leaves alone is
the base case byte for byte), cases gain prefetch-reachable reads, mutation
blocks (lets, `require`, `if`, several writes to `key` and `k2`, reads after
writes), a prelude of mutations `prep0..` run in order on the same store before
`run` (`Case::steps`, sometimes as the other viewer; `observe` then reads both
keys), and expiry (`expire at .until`, a clock advancing from `seeded_at`
through each step to `clock`, sweeps, `until: now + ttl`). Every oracle runs the
prelude (`execute::world`) and compares its outcomes (`prelude-*` fields;
`store-postgres-prelude`); a device acquires at the seed clock and catches up by
one sync round after the server's prelude. The prelude is emitted first so no
rewrite moves its sites. `lean::generate` narrows half the cases outside the
model (projections, expiry) back into its fragment; the rest stay named
unsupported (`shape`, `loop`, `table-expiry`). The report's `coverage` counts
each new feature (`block/*`, `prelude/*`, `projection/*`, `expire/*`, `clock/*`).

Quarry device testing (`src/devices.rs`, `src/device_writes.rs`, LLP 3000.035
§5.1 oracle 4): each case runs on a server engine and on a device of each store
(native SQLite at a file path, and memory), both brought up through the client's
own path: the `/schema` backend as JSON text, then `local.ts::syncRound` (capture
`sync_state`, `/sync` snapshot pages built as `serve_sync.rs` builds them,
`observe_store`, `apply` through `call_text`, catch-up stream). Never seed a
device store directly. Reads the device publishes without the server (`complete`
with `unknown` false, or a known refusal) compare the whole outcome record
except `seq` (and server-only `delivery`); every other read counts under a named
reason (`online-only-*`). Low ceilings reach the device only through `call`'s
`spent` ledger (`default - n`), compared at the server's boundary positions.
Writes go through `admit`: a device refusal must equal the server's, a
predicted row must equal the committed one (less its `pending` marker), and
after `settle` plus a sync round the device must answer `observe` as the server
does. `run-devices` accepts `--seconds`. Known findings are retained under
`cases/found/device-*` with a `DIAGNOSIS.md`; `tests/devices.rs` replays them
and `tests/smoke.rs` pins the fixed-seed signature set.

The Lean oracle (`run-lean`, LLP 3000.035 §5.2): `snapback4_lang::emit::lean`
translates each generated program's surface tree (never the binder's IR) to
the model's case JSON; `quarry-oracle` (`lean/Oracle.lean`, one process per
worker) answers one tagged outcome per line; the engine runs on SQLite at
default ceilings. Compared: kind, refusal code, data (tagged: an id is not a
text), `complete`, `next` presence, each side's own continuation, and a
mutation's `observe` reread. Not compared: meter fields and sites. Programs
outside the model's fragment are named unsupported reasons, never passes.
`lean::generate` adds Lean-only variants (per-effect rules, a hidden probe
target, nullable/id orders, residual filters) aimed at `lean/OPEN.md`. A
disagreement is a Rust defect, a model defect (only when a document
contradicts the model: fix the model, rerun `lean/check.sh`), or an open
point (record the engine's behaviour in `OPEN.md`); never change the model to
match the engine without a document. Findings live in
`cases/found/lean--*` with `DIAGNOSIS.md`; `tests/lean.rs` replays them and
pins the open signature set. Lean-dependent tests skip loudly without the
built model; `SNAPBACK4_REQUIRE_LEAN=1` makes that a failure.

Quarry scheduled testing: prepare a separate clean clone whose current branch
can fast-forward to `origin/main`. The runner fetches/fast-forwards it and starts
its own socket-only evaluation cluster with `fsync=off`,
`synchronous_commit=off`, `full_page_writes=off`, `shared_buffers=1GB`, and
`max_connections=200`. It builds the Lean model's `quarry-oracle` first
(`lake build` in `lean/`; a host without `lake` fails the run), then runs the
SQLite, store, device and Lean campaigns on the selected
seed window, replays shrunk findings twice, and files/appends open `issues/*.md`
tickets. Only ticket changes are committed/pushed, with three fetch/rebase
retries. State and reports live outside Git under `--state-dir`; seeds advance
only after completed work and successful owned-cluster cleanup. Dirty/diverged
trees, overlap, setup failures, partial reports and failed publication refuse.
A crash can retain the lock and cluster directory; inspect `lock/owner.json`
and the run report. Never stop a cluster by process name.

`--dry-run --cases 32` writes previews and a separate `next-seed.dry-run.json`,
never commits or pushes. Dry runs may explicitly supply `--external-postgres
'host=... user=... dbname=...'`; that cluster is neither started nor stopped by
the runner. Ambient connections do not override normal private-cluster ownership.
The installer remains an operator action; do not invoke it during development.

The Lean oracle on a scheduled host (one-time, per user, no sudo): install
elan with `curl https://raw.githubusercontent.com/leanprover/elan/master/elan-init.sh -sSf | sh -s -- -y --default-toolchain none`.
The runner finds `lake` on `PATH` or in `~/.elan/bin`; the first `lake build`
downloads the toolchain pinned in `lean/lean-toolchain` (Lean 4.34.1, no
Mathlib), so the first scheduled run needs network. Check by hand with
`(cd lean && ./check.sh)` in the runner's clone. `run.json` records the pinned
toolchain and the oracle binary's digest.

# llp-orient

<!-- @ref https://github.com/ccheever/llp/blob/v0.5.2/llp/0008-distributed-agent-skills.rfc.md#the-skills--core-five-plus-flags — skill contract -->
<!-- @ref https://github.com/ccheever/llp/blob/v0.5.2/llp/0000-linked-literate-programming.explainer.md#2-reference-syntax — the grammar this reads -->

Load the design rationale governing the area you're about to touch, before touching it. This is the consumption side of LLP — orienting first is what turns `@ref` from a possibility into a habit.

Invoke as `/llp-orient <path-or-task>` (file, directory, subsystem, or task description). With no argument, use the current change scope (`git diff --name-only`).

## Trigger

Start of a coding task that touches an area with documented design. The orient-first *rule* lives in the repo's always-loaded `AGENTS.md`; this skill is the detailed form that rule routes to for non-trivial work — don't rely on ambient description-match alone.

## Invariants

- MUST be read-only: never edit code or LLP documents.
- MUST scope to the task: pull the constraints that bind this change, not a corpus summary.
- MUST cite, not restate: reference policy as `LLP NNNN#anchor` rather than paraphrasing sections.
- MUST NOT invent constraints. If something isn't documented, say so.
- MUST NOT guess the rationale behind a broken `@ref` — report it and hand off to `llp-maintain`.

## Workflow

> **Recipe (advisory)** — corpus root = nearest `llp/` walking up from the path in scope (LLP 0001 §Unit of adoption); "no corpus" is an empty walk, not cwd-relative absence. Read `llp/foundation/` first (the kernel; the root LLP is there once `Active` — on a fresh adoption it is still `Draft` and sits in `current/`), then skim `llp/current/` (the declared working set); both hold relative links — symlinks, or small files containing the relative path — so `ls` answers and either form resolves. Then the root LLP's systems vocabulary. `Grep '@ref'` over the files in scope and `Read` each target — these are the highest-signal context. `Grep` the `**Systems:**` headers across `llp/` for LLPs governing the subsystems touched; read the governing ones (Spec, Decision, Principles, relevant RFCs). Follow `Related:` and nearby `@ref` targets one hop out; stop there unless something is clearly load-bearing. Stale-pin nudge (skip offline): read the installed tag from the skills receipt (or a `source:` pin), compare `git ls-remote --tags <origin>` — if upstream is newer, say so in one line ("skills at v0.3.0, upstream v0.5.2 — run /llp-adopt update") and move on; never update from here.

## Artifact

A context pack with these sections, faster to read than the documents it points at:

- **Scope** — files / subsystems queried
- **Governing LLPs** — numbered list, one-line gloss each; mark which are in `foundation/` and which are in `current/` (in-flight — its guidance may still move)
- **Active constraints** — bullets, each citing `LLP NNNN#anchor`
- **Relevant rationale** — the *why*, same citation form
- **Open questions / risks** for this area
- **Likely doc updates** if the change lands
- **Hand-off** — one of `none | llp-adopt | llp-create | llp-review | llp-maintain`

## Hand-offs

- No corpus (no `llp/` walking up from the path in scope) → `llp-adopt`.
- The task needs a decision no LLP covers → `llp-create` (or `llp-review` if proposal-shaped).
- An `@ref` target or anchor doesn't resolve → `llp-maintain`.

//! Historical row transforms, separate from application-callable programs.
// @ref LLP 3000.000.000.002#2-language — the compiler and raw-schema boundary share a closed expression fragment.
use super::*;
use crate::ir::{Builtin, Expr, Program, ProgramKind, Stmt};
#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
pub struct Backfill {
    pub table: String,
    pub name: String,
    pub inputs: BTreeMap<String, ColumnType>,
    pub outputs: BTreeSet<String>,
    pub program: Program,
}
impl Backfill {
    pub fn key(&self) -> String { digest(&self.normalized()) }
    pub fn normalized(&self) -> Self {
        // Locations are diagnostic metadata, never transformation identity.
        let mut normalized = self.clone();
        fn expr(e: &mut Expr) {
            match e {
                Expr::Get(r) | Expr::Exists(r) => { r.site.clear(); for e in &mut r.key { expr(e); } }
                Expr::Scan(r) => {
                    r.site.clear();
                    for k in &mut r.prefix { if let crate::ir::IndexKeyExpr::Scalar(e)=k {expr(e);} }
                    if let Some(e)=&mut r.filter {expr(e);}
                    if let Some(r)=&mut r.range {for e in [&mut r.gt,&mut r.gte,&mut r.lt,&mut r.lte].into_iter().flatten(){expr(e);}}
                }
                Expr::Field { base, .. } | Expr::Unary { value: base, .. } => expr(base),
                Expr::Array(es) | Expr::Builtin { args: es, .. } => { for e in es { expr(e); } }
                Expr::Object(fs) => { for e in fs.values_mut() { expr(e); } }
                Expr::Shape { base, fields } => { expr(base); for e in fields.values_mut() { expr(e); } }
                Expr::Binary { left, right, .. } => { expr(left); expr(right); }
                Expr::Block { body, value } => { statements(body); expr(value); }
                _ => (),
            }
        }
        fn statements(body: &mut [Stmt]) {
            for s in body {
                match s {
                    Stmt::Let { value, .. } | Stmt::Const { value, .. } | Stmt::Assign { value, .. } | Stmt::Return(value) => expr(value),
                    Stmt::Require { condition, site, .. } => { *site=None; expr(condition); },
                    Stmt::If { condition, then_body, else_body } => { expr(condition); statements(then_body); statements(else_body); }
                    _ => (),
                }
            }
        }
        statements(&mut normalized.program.body);
        normalized
    }
    pub fn admit(&self, schema: &Schema) -> Result<(), String> {
        let table = schema.table(&self.table).ok_or("backfill names an absent table")?;
        if table.ephemeral.is_some() || schema.maintain_for(&self.table).is_some() { return Err("backfill requires an authoritative durable table".into()); }
        if self.program.kind != ProgramKind::Query || self.program.native || self.outputs.is_empty() { return Err("backfill must produce a nonempty object patch".into()); }
        for c in &self.outputs {
            let kind = table.columns.get(c).ok_or_else(||format!("backfill output {}.{c} is not declared",self.table))?;
            if c=="id" || table.engine_field(c) || table.maintained.contains_key(c) || table.expire.as_ref()==Some(c) || table.not_before.as_ref()==Some(c) || !scalar(kind) {
                return Err(format!("backfill output {}.{c} must be an authoritative scalar, not an asset, lifetime or captured field",self.table));
            }
        }
        if !body(&self.program.body,schema) { return Err("backfill expressions allow deterministic scalar transforms, unique point lookups and bounded existence; no viewer, clock, ids, row collections, loops or writes".into()); }
        Ok(())
    }
}
fn scalar(k: &ColumnType) -> bool {
    match k {
        ColumnType::Optional(k) => scalar(k),
        ColumnType::Image { .. } | ColumnType::Video { .. } | ColumnType::File { .. } | ColumnType::List { .. } | ColumnType::ListRange { .. } => false,
        _ => true,
    }
}
fn body(stmts: &[Stmt], s: &Schema) -> bool {
    stmts.iter().all(|stmt| match stmt {
        Stmt::Let { value, .. } | Stmt::Const { value, .. } | Stmt::Assign { value, .. } | Stmt::Return(value) => expr(value,s),
        Stmt::Require { condition, .. } => expr(condition,s),
        Stmt::If { condition, then_body, else_body } => expr(condition,s) && body(then_body,s) && body(else_body,s),
        _ => false,
    })
}
fn expr(e: &Expr, s: &Schema) -> bool {
    match e {
        Expr::Literal(_) | Expr::Variable(_) => true,
        Expr::Input(_) | Expr::Merge(_) => false,
        Expr::Scan(r) => r.existence && r.after.is_none() && r.before.is_none() && r.prefix.iter().all(|k| k.scalar().is_some_and(|e|expr(e,s))) && r.filter.as_ref().is_none_or(|e|expr(e,s)) && r.range.as_ref().is_none_or(|r|[&r.gt,&r.gte,&r.lt,&r.lte].iter().all(|e|e.as_ref().is_none_or(|e|expr(e,s)))),
        Expr::Field { base, .. } | Expr::Unary { value: base, .. } => expr(base,s),
        Expr::Array(es) => es.iter().all(|e|expr(e,s)),
        Expr::Object(es) => es.values().all(|e|expr(e,s)),
        Expr::Shape { base, fields } => expr(base,s) && fields.values().all(|e|expr(e,s)),
        Expr::Binary { left, right, .. } => expr(left,s) && expr(right,s),
        Expr::Block { body: stmts, value } => body(stmts,s) && expr(value,s),
        Expr::Get(r) | Expr::Exists(r) => s.table(&r.table).is_some_and(|t| t.index(&r.index).is_some_and(|i|i.unique && i.is_plain() && i.components.len()==r.key.len())) && r.key.iter().all(|e|expr(e,s)),
        Expr::Builtin { builtin, args } => matches!(builtin,
            Builtin::RequireUncapped | Builtin::ProjectFields { .. } | Builtin::OptionalField { .. } | Builtin::Len | Builtin::Count | Builtin::Slice | Builtin::Concat | Builtin::Lower | Builtin::Trim | Builtin::Text(_) | Builtin::Sha256 | Builtin::First | Builtin::Last | Builtin::At | Builtin::Min | Builtin::Max | Builtin::Divide { .. } | Builtin::Civil(_) | Builtin::Calendar { .. }
        ) && args.len()==builtin.arity() && args.iter().all(|e|expr(e,s)),
    }
}
//! Server activation builder; all fallible work precedes commit.
use super::*;
impl Engine {
    pub(super) fn activate(
        store: &mut Store,
        schema: &Schema,
        programs: &BTreeMap<String, Program>,
        release: Option<&release::ReleaseActivation>,
        now: i64,
        allow_feed_adoption: bool,
        expected_release: Option<u64>,
        expected_backend: Option<&crate::store::opening::Backend>,
    ) -> Result<(u64, crate::store::opening::Metadata), RuntimeError> {
        schema.validate_column_owners()?;
        for program in programs.values() {
            crate::schema::ephemeral::admit_program(schema, program)?;
            admission::check_state_predicates(schema, program)?;
        }
        for program in programs.values() {
            for table in crate::ir::read_sites(program)
                .into_iter()
                .map(|s| s.table)
                .chain(crate::ir::write_tables(program).into_iter().map(|(t, _)| t))
            {
                schema.refuse_private(&table)?;
            }
        }
        for schedule in &schema.schedules {
            if !schedule.valid()
            {
                return Err(RuntimeError::Schema(format!(
                    "schedule {} has an invalid period or offset",
                    schedule.name
                )));
            }
            let Some(program) = programs
                .get(&schedule.target)
                .filter(|p| matches!(p.kind, ProgramKind::Job | ProgramKind::Action))
            else {
                return Err(RuntimeError::Schema(format!(
                    "schedule {} names missing job or action {}; restore or remove the schedule",
                    schedule.name, schedule.target
                )));
            };
            if program.kind == ProgramKind::Action {
                let seat = schedule
                    .service
                    .as_deref()
                    .filter(|seat| crate::identity::is_service(seat))
                    .ok_or_else(|| {
                        RuntimeError::Schema(
                            "a scheduled action requires its declared service seat".into(),
                        )
                    })?;
                actions::authorize(program, seat)?;
            } else if schedule.service.is_some() {
                return Err(RuntimeError::Schema(
                    "a scheduled job cannot name a service seat".into(),
                ));
            }
            if program.args.iter().any(|(name, kind)| {
                !matches!(name.as_str(), "periodStart" | "periodEnd")
                    || *kind != crate::ir::ArgType::Time
            }) {
                return Err(RuntimeError::Schema(
                    "scheduled work takes periodStart: time and periodEnd: time, or nothing".into(),
                ));
            }
        }
        let prior_items = std::cell::RefCell::new(Vec::new());
        let population=std::cell::Cell::new(None);
        let outcome=store.activate_at_with_launch(schema, Some(programs), false, now, allow_feed_adoption, |conn| {
            Store::check_selected_release(conn, expected_release)?;
            if let Some(backend) = expected_backend { backend.check(conn)?; }
            if let Some(options)=release {
                options.check(conn)?;
                *prior_items.borrow_mut()=crate::store::release_history::inventory(conn,None,usize::MAX)?;
                crate::store::dormant::enable(conn)?;
                conn.execute("INSERT INTO _meta(key,value) VALUES('release_native_digest',?1) ON CONFLICT(key) DO UPDATE SET value=excluded.value",[options.native_digest.as_deref().unwrap_or("")])?;
                conn.execute("INSERT INTO _meta(key,value) VALUES('activation_restore',?1) ON CONFLICT(key) DO UPDATE SET value=excluded.value",[if options.restore_dormant {"true"}else{"false"}])?;
            }
            Ok(())
        }, |tx, changed, report| {
            let now=now.max(tx.time_audience_clock()?);
            if let Some(options)=release {tx.set_meta("release",&options.release.to_string())?;}
            use rusqlite::OptionalExtension;
            let repair_live_sums = tx.conn().query_row("SELECT value FROM _meta WHERE key = 'live_sum_candidates'", [], |row| row.get::<_, String>(0)).optional()?.as_deref() != Some("1");
            let previous: Option<String> = tx.conn().query_row("SELECT schema FROM _generations WHERE generation = (SELECT CAST(value AS INTEGER) FROM _meta WHERE key = 'generation')", [], |row| row.get(0)).optional()?;
            let previous: Option<Schema> = previous.map(|text| serde_json::from_str(&text).map_err(|e| RuntimeError::decode(e, RuntimeError::Store))).transpose()?;
            let needs_maintained_id_marker = (schema.maintains.iter().any(|maintain| maintain.row_key.is_none())
                || previous.as_ref().is_some_and(|old| old.maintains.iter().any(|maintain| maintain.row_key.is_none())))
                && tx.meta(crate::schema::MAINTAINED_ID_META)?.as_deref() != Some(crate::schema::MAINTAINED_ID_VERSION);
            if !changed && !repair_live_sums && !needs_maintained_id_marker { return Ok(()); }
            let repair_maintained_ids = needs_maintained_id_marker
                && previous.as_ref().is_some_and(|old| old.maintains.iter().any(|maintain| maintain.row_key.is_none()));
            if changed {
                let epoch = tx.next_seq()?;
                for table in partition::changed_group_audiences(previous.as_ref(), schema) {
                    tx.set_meta(&partition::audience_epoch_key(&table), &epoch.to_string())?;
                    tx.set_meta(&partition::audience_epoch_time_key(&table), &now.to_string())?;
                }
            }
            let population_changed=tx.meta("activation_population")?.as_deref()==Some("true");
            let rebuild: Vec<_> = schema.maintains.iter().filter(|m| {
                (changed && (population_changed || previous.as_ref().is_none_or(|old| crate::successor::maintain_rebuild_needed(old, schema, m))))
                    || (repair_maintained_ids && m.row_key.is_none())
                    || (repair_live_sums && matches!(m.kind, crate::schema::MaintainKind::Sum { .. }) && schema.table(&m.source).is_some_and(|source| source.is_expiring()))
            }).collect();
            tx.set_meta("live_sum_candidates", "1")?;
            if needs_maintained_id_marker {
                // This write is inside the activation transaction: a failed
                // rebuild leaves both old rows and the old/missing marker. A
                // first aggregate has no legacy target identity to rewrite,
                // but records the current address function for later opens.
                tx.set_meta(crate::schema::MAINTAINED_ID_META, crate::schema::MAINTAINED_ID_VERSION)?;
            }
            let attribution = recorded::paths_changed(schema, previous.as_ref());
            let columns = column_activation::changed_parents(schema, if population_changed {None} else {previous.as_ref()});
            if rebuild.is_empty() && !attribution && columns.is_empty() && !repair_maintained_ids { return Ok(()); }
            let seq = tx.next_seq()?;
            let phase = std::time::Instant::now();
            if attribution {
                // This runner owns only activation rewrites; ordinary rebuilds
                // below retain their commit and partition accounting.
                let mut capture = Runner::new(tx, schema, Context::system().at(now), Meter::new(Ceilings::unbounded(), Cut::Abort), seq);
                capture.capture_activation(previous.as_ref(), Some(report))?;
                report.detail("capture recorded paths", phase);
                // A newly introduced aggregate has no legacy identity map to
                // preserve. Keep its event-free recorded-path rebuild out of
                // commit accounting, as activation did before the address
                // migration existed.
                if !repair_maintained_ids {
                    capture.rebuild_recorded_activation(previous.as_ref(), &rebuild.iter().copied().filter(|m| m.private.is_none()).collect::<Vec<_>>(), report)?;
                }
            }
            let mut runner = Runner::new(tx, schema, Context::system().at(now), Meter::new(Ceilings::unbounded(), Cut::Abort), seq);
            if repair_maintained_ids {
                let started = std::time::Instant::now();
                let rewritten = runner.rewrite_legacy_maintained_references(&rebuild.iter().copied().cloned().collect::<Vec<_>>(), previous.as_ref(), Some(tx))?;
                if rewritten != 0 { report.changes.push(format!("rewrote {rewritten} legacy aggregate references")); }
                report.detail("rewrite legacy aggregate references", started);
            }
            // Legacy maps must be captured before any activation rebuild can
            // remove their target rows. Recorded values themselves are
            // stamped first so their historical paths may still traverse the
            // predecessor addresses.
            if attribution && repair_maintained_ids {
                runner.rebuild_recorded_activation(previous.as_ref(), &rebuild.iter().copied().filter(|m| m.private.is_none()).collect::<Vec<_>>(), report)?;
            }
            runner.activate_columns(previous.as_ref(), &rebuild, &columns, Some(report))?;
            for maintain in rebuild.into_iter().filter(|m| m.private.is_none()) {
                if !recorded::table_paths_changed(schema.table(&maintain.source), previous.as_ref().and_then(|s| s.table(&maintain.source))) {
                    runner.rebuild_maintain_reported(maintain, report)?;
                }
            }
            let events = runner.events.take();
            let tables: Vec<String> = runner.written_tables.iter().cloned().collect();
            let written = runner.rows_written;
            drop(runner);
            if written > 0 || !columns.is_empty() {
                tx.record_commit(seq, now, "rebuild", crate::SYSTEM_PRINCIPAL, "rebuild", &tables, written)?;
                partition::append(tx, seq, &events)?;
            }
            Ok(())
        }, |tx| {
            if let Some(options)=release {
                crate::store::release_history::finalize_restorations(tx)?;
                let mut items=crate::store::release_history::inventory(tx.conn(),None,usize::MAX)?;
                let dormant_items=items.len();
                let mut restored_items=0usize;
                for item in &mut items {
                    let before=prior_items.borrow().iter().find(|i|i.key==item.key).cloned();
                    let count=before.as_ref().map_or(0,|i|i.rows);
                    item.before_rows=Some(count);item.after_rows=Some(item.rows);
                    item.before_sample_ids=Some(before.map_or_else(Vec::new,|i|i.sample_ids));
                    let restored=if item.kind=="rows" {
                        tx.meta(&format!("activation_narrowed_restored:{}",item.path))?.and_then(|s|s.parse::<u64>().ok()).unwrap_or(0)
                    } else if item.kind=="column" {tx.meta(&format!("activation_column_restored:{}",item.path))?.and_then(|s|s.parse::<u64>().ok()).unwrap_or(0)} else {0};
                    item.restored_rows=Some(restored);
                    if restored>0 {item.action="partially restored".into();restored_items+=1;}
                }
                let remaining:std::collections::BTreeSet<_>=items.iter().map(|i|i.key.clone()).collect();
                for mut item in prior_items.borrow().iter().filter(|i|!remaining.contains(&i.key)).cloned() {
                    let restored=if matches!(item.kind.as_str(),"rows"|"column") {
                        let prefix=if item.kind=="rows" {"activation_narrowed_restored"} else {"activation_column_restored"};
                        tx.meta(&format!("{prefix}:{}",item.path))?.and_then(|s|s.parse::<u64>().ok()).unwrap_or(0)
                    } else if item.kind=="table" {
                        tx.meta(&format!("activation_table_restored:{}",item.path))?.and_then(|s|s.parse::<u64>().ok()).unwrap_or(0)
                    } else {item.rows};
                    item.action=if restored>0 || item.definition_only {"restored"} else {"removed without restoration"}.into();
                    item.before_rows=Some(item.rows);item.after_rows=Some(0);item.restored_rows=Some(restored);item.before_sample_ids=Some(item.sample_ids.clone());
                    if restored>0 || item.definition_only {restored_items+=1;}
                    items.push(item);
                }
                population.set(Some((tx.meta("activation_narrowed_retained")?.and_then(|s|s.parse::<u64>().ok()).unwrap_or(0),tx.meta("activation_narrowed_restored")?.and_then(|s|s.parse::<u64>().ok()).unwrap_or(0))));
                crate::store::release_history::event(tx,&options.operation,Some(options.release),options.expected_previous,serde_json::json!({"dormant_items":dormant_items,"restored_items":restored_items,"native_digest":options.native_digest,"narrowed_retained":tx.meta("activation_narrowed_retained")?.and_then(|s|s.parse::<u64>().ok()).unwrap_or(0),"narrowed_restored":tx.meta("activation_narrowed_restored")?.and_then(|s|s.parse::<u64>().ok()).unwrap_or(0)}),&items)?;
                tx.conn().execute("DELETE FROM _meta WHERE key IN ('activation_restore','activation_population','activation_narrowed_retained','activation_narrowed_restored') OR key LIKE 'activation_narrowed_restored:%' OR key LIKE 'activation_column_restored:%' OR key LIKE 'activation_table_restored:%' OR key LIKE 'activation_table_restored_ids:%' OR key LIKE 'activation_narrowed_restored_ids:%' OR key LIKE 'activation_column_restored_ids:%'",[])?;
            }
            crate::store::opening::Metadata::read(tx)
        });
        if outcome.is_ok() {
            if let Some((retained,restored))=population.get().filter(|(a,b)|*a>0 || *b>0) {
                store.adoption_report.changes.insert(0,format!("{retained} newly dormant {}; {restored} narrowed {} restored", if retained == 1 { "row" } else { "rows" }, if restored == 1 { "row" } else { "rows" }));
            }
        }
        if outcome.is_ok() {
            if let Ok(items)=store.dormant_inventory(None,usize::MAX) {
                if release.is_some() { store.adoption_report.changes.insert(0, crate::store::adoption_report::AdoptionReport::dormant(&items)); }
            }
        }
        outcome
    }
}
//! A development reset: the adopted schema over an empty store, under a new
//! store identity, optionally reseeded, in one SQLite transaction.
// @ref LLP 3000.007#20.25 — what "fresh" keeps and what it empties; why the head keeps counting.
use super::*;

/// Tables a reset keeps whole: the store's own metadata, the catalog that
/// numbers tables and indexes, and the adopted generations.
const KEPT: [&str; 3] = ["_meta", "_catalog", "_generations"];

/// Identity tables a reset empties except for service seats: the dev
/// worker's session and `snapback4 token` seats outlive a reset, accounts,
/// guests and their sessions do not.
const SERVICE_SEATS: [(&str, &str); 5] = [
    ("_principals", "id"), ("_sessions", "principal"), ("_read_sessions", "principal"),
    ("_principal_generations", "principal"), ("_session_mint_bindings", "principal"),
];

/// What a development reset did. The caller unlinks `assets` after the
/// commit, outside any engine guard it holds.
#[derive(Debug)]
pub struct DevReset {
    pub previous_store_id: String,
    pub store_id: String,
    /// The store's head after the reset and any reseed.
    pub seq: u64,
    pub seeded: u64,
    pub assets: assets::AssetSweep,
}

impl Engine {
    /// Empty every application table and everything derived from them
    /// (views, maintained columns, indexes, counts, the partition log),
    /// every queue (effects, jobs, actions), uploaded assets and every
    /// non-service account and session; keep the adopted schema and
    /// generation. The store takes a new identity so a connected device
    /// re-acquires instead of keeping its partition; the head keeps counting
    /// (the reset is itself a commit). `rows` are then written as `/seed`
    /// writes them, in the same transaction. Development only: the release
    /// host never calls this.
    pub fn dev_reset_at(&mut self, rows: Vec<(String, Value)>, now: i64) -> Result<DevReset, RuntimeError> {
        // Like adoption: no ephemeral program runs across the identity change.
        drop(self.ephemeral.lock()?);
        let tx = self.store.transaction()?;
        // @ref LLP 3000.000.000.000.000#2-fixture-semantics — the new store identity must not inherit the retired store's future clock.
        tx.conn().execute("DELETE FROM _meta WHERE key = 'time_audience_clock'", [])?;
        let now = time_audience::pin_writer_time(&self.schema, &tx, now)?;
        let previous_store_id = tx.meta("store_id")?.unwrap_or_default();
        let head = tx.next_seq()?.saturating_sub(1);
        let assets: Vec<String> = tx.conn().prepare("SELECT id FROM _assets")?.query_map([], |row| row.get(0))?.collect::<Result<_, _>>()?;
        let tables: Vec<String> = tx.conn()
            .prepare("SELECT name FROM main.sqlite_schema WHERE type = 'table' AND name NOT LIKE 'sqlite\\_%' ESCAPE '\\'")?
            .query_map([], |row| row.get(0))?.collect::<Result<_, _>>()?;
        // @ref LLP 3000.039#6.5 — every table empties together: no referential action runs, and no reference is left naming a removed row.
        for table in &tables {
            if KEPT.contains(&table.as_str()) { continue; }
            let quoted = format!("\"{}\"", table.replace('"', "\"\""));
            match SERVICE_SEATS.iter().find(|(name, _)| name == table) {
                Some((_, column)) => tx.conn().execute(&format!("DELETE FROM {quoted} WHERE substr({column}, 1, ?2) <> ?1"),
                    rusqlite::params![crate::identity::SERVICE_PREFIX, crate::identity::SERVICE_PREFIX.len() as i64])?,
                None => tx.conn().execute(&format!("DELETE FROM {quoted}"), [])?,
            };
        }
        // Prime at the reset clock in this transaction: no old period fires,
        // and the next crossed boundary fires normally. Replay's count restarts.
        tx.conn().execute("DELETE FROM _meta WHERE key LIKE 'schedule:%' OR key LIKE 'schedule-shape:%' OR key = 'dev:suppressed-effects'", [])?;
        for schedule in &self.schema.schedules {
            tx.set_meta(&format!("schedule:{}", schedule.name), &schedule.boundary(now).to_string())?;
            tx.set_meta(&format!("schedule-shape:{}", schedule.name), &crate::value::digest(schedule))?;
        }
        tx.conn().execute("UPDATE _meta SET value = lower(hex(randomblob(16))) WHERE key = 'store_id'", [])?;
        let store_id = tx.meta("store_id")?.unwrap_or_default();
        // Every table changed: no change evidence may certify an interval
        // across the reset.
        tx.changes.borrow_mut().widen();
        let touched: Vec<String> = self.schema.visible_tables().map(|(name, _)| name.clone()).collect();
        tx.record_commit(head + 1, now, "dev reset", crate::SYSTEM_PRINCIPAL, "reset", &touched, 0)?;
        if crate::store::backfills::has_evidence(&tx)? {crate::store::backfills::prepare_inputs(&tx,&self.schema,None)?;}
        let seeded = seed_rows(&self.schema, &tx, rows, now)?;
        let seq = tx.next_seq()?.saturating_sub(1);
        tx.commit()?;
        // A new store identity starts a fresh bounded expiry walk. Clear only
        // after commit so a refused reset keeps the old store's continuation.
        self.expiry_after.clear();
        self.timeline_schedule = timeline::Schedule::default();
        self.ephemeral.rebind_committed(store_id.clone(), self.generation, &self.schema);
        Ok(DevReset { previous_store_id, store_id, seq, seeded, assets: assets::AssetSweep::named(assets, self.store.assets_dir()) })
    }
}
//! Bind historical arguments without requiring them in the current row type.
use super::*;
use crate::{ast, diag::{type_error, unknown}, types::{Ty, scalar_to_column}};
use snapback4_core::schema::backfill::Backfill;
use std::collections::{BTreeMap, BTreeSet};
pub(super) fn bind(catalog: &Catalog, schema: &mut Schema, diagnostics: &mut Vec<Diagnostic>) {
    let mut names = BTreeSet::new(); let mut outputs = BTreeSet::new();
    for d in catalog.documents.iter().flat_map(|d| &d.backfills) {
        if !names.insert((d.table.clone(),d.name.clone())) { diagnostics.push(unknown(&d.site,"duplicate backfill name on this table","give each historical transform its own name")); continue; }
        let decl = ast::ProgramDecl { delivery: None, held:false, predicts:false, server:false, kind:ast::ProgramKind::Query,
            name: format!("backfill{}{}",d.table,d.name), args:d.args.clone(), prelude:vec![],
            body:vec![ast::Stmt::Return { value:d.value.clone(), site:d.site.clone() }], native:false, site:d.site.clone() };
        let Some(bound) = program::Binder::bind(catalog,schema,"schema",&decl,diagnostics) else {continue};
        let Ty::Object(fields) = &bound.result else { diagnostics.push(type_error(&d.site,"backfill returns an object patch","{ column: historicalValue }")); continue; };
        let Some(table) = schema.table(&d.table) else {diagnostics.push(unknown(&d.site,"backfill table is not declared","declare the candidate table"));continue};
        for (c,ty) in fields {
            if let Some(kind) = table.columns.get(c) {
                if !ty.assignable_to(&Ty::from_column(kind)) { diagnostics.push(type_error(&d.site,format!("backfill {}.{c} returns {}, expected {}",d.table,ty.name(),kind),"return a value fitting the candidate column")); }
            }
            if !outputs.insert((d.table.clone(),c.clone())) { diagnostics.push(type_error(&d.site,format!("backfills overlap on {}.{c}",d.table),"combine simultaneous outputs in one backfill")); }
        }
        let fill = Backfill { table:d.table.clone(), name:d.name.clone(), inputs:d.args.iter().map(|a|(a.name.clone(),scalar_to_column(&a.type_))).collect::<BTreeMap<_,_>>(), outputs:fields.keys().cloned().collect(), program:bound.program };
        if let Err(why) = fill.admit(schema) { diagnostics.push(type_error(&d.site,why,"use deterministic historical fields and a scalar patch").guide("deploy")); }
        schema.backfills.push(fill.normalized());
    }
}
