## 10. Wave 10 release lifecycle amendment

Commission: Charlie's REL2 workstream, 2026-10-13; implementer Codex in
sb4-w10-rel2. This amends §9.2's definition of a complete release and §2's
capture restoration. No new language, planner or device protocol is introduced.

A newly packaged numeric source directory carries `.pending-adoption`, written
and fsynced before publication. It is selectable only if database activation
history proves that its adoption committed. Successful deploy removes the marker;
a crash, refused adoption or lost control reply leaves it. `latest`, numeric
rollback, release inspection and check-release all use this rule. The database
proof recovers a committed activation even if pointer/ledger completion failed,
and recognizes its committed predecessor as proof of legacy activation.
For retained stores, committed history gates unmarked directories too, excluding
refused candidates from older binaries. Legacy stores without release history
keep their complete-source selection rule. The marker is excluded from the
source digest. All production writes of numeric
release source go through deploy; rollback and serve only select existing source.
A refused candidate remains a reserved identity and can never become a selectable
release merely because it compiles. A matching-source dormant-name refusal leads
with `rollback <dir> --to latest`; other source uses `--restore-dormant`. Human
retention evidence lists counts/samples rather than serialized inventory JSON.

Bare rollback selects the previous release of the last committed activation,
including rollback and roll-forward; prune and same-release selections are excluded. Thus v1 → v2 → v3 → v1
followed by bare rollback activates v3. An explicit --to overrides this. Legacy
stores without activation history require explicit --to. A no-op adds no event.

`deploy --check` runs the exact candidate activation on the private snapshot with
**deploy** restoration intent, while rollback/check-release previews select
restoration. No candidate is selectable and live state remains unchanged.
Preview validates static worker prerequisites without starting workers. Successful
constraint narration comes from the validated candidate after dormant projection,
not the predecessor population. Successful previews describe dormant rows/cells with counts and bounded ids,
report successful maintain rebuild source visits, and omit speculative adoption
failures and capture-fill promises. Compatibility warnings are advisory, only for
populated affected tables, and include their current row count (not a prediction
that every row is incompatible). Preview flags removed schedules and populated
removed application tables as business safeguards absent from the target. Quarry
has no hold declaration: every removed populated table gets this advisory, so a
hold's name cannot evade it. Existing live writes can change any preview result.

Retired engine capture columns retain their source table definition alongside
cells. Restoring the same read-through definition uses the preserved capture and
parent lifetime, even when the intervening release widened its read rule. The
predecessor audience correspondence barrier applies only to rows requiring new
captures. Historical rows must still pass retained reference lifetime and
capture/parent validation. A changed retained read-through definition refuses;
new rows without historical captures retain the existing first-adoption checks.
Source expiry is evaluated before projection, so restoration cannot revive an
expired captured lifetime. Recorded attribution keeps its historical value.

`run <job> --release <dir>` routes to the served store at its canonical loopback
address using `SNAPBACK4_SERVICE_TOKEN` or `--token-env NAME`; it needs no local
project/source. `POST /jobs/<job>` requires a full service capability, rechecked
under the enqueueing engine lock, and executes system work on the existing FIFO.
Every full service seat is privileged to trigger any system job; operators must
provision these tokens accordingly. Accounts, guests, read scopes and personas
are refused. `/dev/jobs` stays development-only. The reply waits for done/parked;
a lost reply is ambiguous and does not authorize automatic retry. It adds no job
priority, worker pool, token scope or idempotency contract.

`serve.lock` remains an address hint. Only successful sidecar ownership allows
reclamation. After acquiring it, an ESRCH result from kill(pid,0) identifies a dead
record and emits a diagnostic with PID, URL and directory before overwriting it.
A live/reused PID or uncertain probe never authorizes signalling or lock bypass.

Acceptance: v1/v2/rollback/refused-v3/latest and explicit refused selection;
non-numeric-history rollback; deploy preview state equality; empty/populated
warnings; all packaged rollback claims; old release E_OP/NOT_FOUND/null and exact
forward values; retained captures after widening plus deleted/reused parent and
new-row refusal; service-token job completion and unauthorized/read-only requests;
stale-lock recovery and live-owner conflict provenance. HS owns handshake/types;
DX2 owns starters/client recipes; DIAG and PAGE2 own language/planner diagnostics.


REL2 review round one: fresh GPT-6.1-sol found legacy predecessor selection,
pre-dormancy constraint narration and omitted static worker prerequisite checks.
All three were reproduced and corrected with migration, unique/narrowing and
worker-selection regressions. Review artifacts retain the verbatim findings.


REL2 round two: [Muse](./reviews/3000.000.000-rel2-wave10-r2.muse.md) and
[fresh Codex](./reviews/3000.000.000-rel2-wave10-r2.codex.md) both READY on
this amendment and implementation. Focused final verification passes. Informational
receipt sampling also covers populated items beyond empty inventory definitions;
guide consistency lives in its own test module, preserving the pre-existing
parent test file's line count. No publication or push was performed by REL2.

## 11. Wave 11 factual release narration

Commission and implementer: Charlie Cheever's NARR workstream, Codex,
2026-10-14; target 0.4.28. This amends §§9.2 and 10's narration, not retention
or admission policy. Independent readiness reviews cover this section together
with LLP 3000.011.003.000 §11 and LLP 3000.001.000's NARR diagnostics.

Compatibility is directional. A narrowing warning counts active row images
whose named column value fails the candidate's same type/text/asset predicate
used in narrowed-row activation; it does not count table population or
reference-closed dependents. Literal defaults and optional nulls are materialized
under the current schema. Values are bounded, de-duplicated samples. Missing
required fields remain a backfill refusal, not a dormant-value warning.
Zero incompatible values produce a note without a population count. Deploy
widening describes values future writes could use and the previous release
that cannot hold them; forward selection of saved source emits no widening
warning. Counts describe the read snapshot at narration time; concurrent
writes can change a later activation. Preview still performs exact activation
on its coherent private snapshot and changes no live state.

Scheduled behavior compares the schedule definition and lexical authored job
source, excluding comments, whitespace and source positions, before comparing
compiled programs. Missing job/schedule, edited schedule and edited job body
are distinct warnings. An unchanged body whose compiled program changes gets
a dependency note naming changed read/written tables and added/removed columns
when available. Missing saved source does not justify claiming a job absent.
Rollback preview also lists operations absent in the candidate and current
rule clauses differing from its rule. These are behavior differences to review,
not claims that every changed rule weakens enforcement. Removed table populations
are named without guessing whether a generic table is a hold/safeguard.

Receipts report total current dormant rows (whole-table and narrowed), cells
and logical index entries separately, and narrowed restorations separately.
Samples are sorted, de-duplicated and labelled by kind/path, at most five ids
per path. FIX28 makes row and cell populations disjoint: whole-table/narrowed dormant
rows include any separately retained cells of those same ids. Column counts and
samples include only retained cells of still-active rows. Covered cell bytes and
assets belong to their dormant row item; definitions with zero standalone cells
remain inventoried for restoration. Index entries are an independent logical
unit. The archived-row rollback example is one row and two cells, with disjoint
sample ids. Restoration counters still record actual consumed cells, including
covered cells, independently of the current inventory counts.
Per-column preview steps use those same standalone cell counts. A removed
column with zero standalone cells says no standalone cells become dormant,
explaining that cells of any dormant rows count with their rows; it never says
"its cells become dormant (0 cells)". The many-column enum-narrowing fixture
has one incompatible row and no active rows: its correct plan is one row and
zero cells. Adding a compatible row yields one row and twenty standalone cells,
one per removed column, with only the compatible row in each column's samples.
Retained indexes are definitions, not physical dormant keys; inventory computes
the entries those definitions represent over current/retained images, including
word-token fanout and dormant cells, rather than labelling row population as
entries. The existing inventory JSON field `rows` retains its count slot;
`kind` determines its unit. Human column/index previews use cells/entries.

Graceful shutdown unlinks only the address file inode this host opened, after
release-control drains, while retaining its ownership sidecar and lock. Owner
drop also clears its own address inode. A replaced address record is preserved.
Dead-owner reclaim is silent normally and a note under --verbose, without an
old URL that could be mistaken for new readiness. Busy live ownership still
refuses with PID/address/directory. Fatal signals remain immediate.

The deploy card distinguishes bare-null point queries, explicit `! else CODE`
guards (including queries), ignored added columns and compatible forward.
Its rollback client recipe pairs generated types with selected server source,
or requires runtime field/operation compatibility handling in a shared client.
Preview names behavior differences beside dormant counts because storage
restoration cannot restore product checks missing from selected source.

Acceptance: one CLI/HTTP regression seeds three draft rows, widens an enum,
adds a literal-default column to a scheduled sweep, sets one archived value,
SIGTERMs serve, previews/rolls back and forwards. Assert exact warning/note,
kind counts, labelled samples, old-source field/guard behavior and lock cleanup;
check the guide's matching text. Separate controls cover actual job/schedule
changes, missing operations/rules, words-index fanout, empty incompatible
populations, verbose dead reclaim and preserved live ownership.

## 12. Wave 12 release-spanning clients and clause comparison

### 12.1 Release-spanning clients

REL3's commissioned served-capability/catalog, generated compatibility,
rollback dependency preview and development evidence amendment is
[LLP 3000.000.000.000](./3000.000.000.000-release-spanning-clients-and-development-contracts.rfc.md).
It replaces §11's application-owned shared-client field/operation guards with
a typed opt-in SDK surface; retained storage and execution authority stay here.

### 12.2 Clause comparison

Implementer: Codex for Charlie Cheever, SURF, 2026-10-14, target 0.4.29.
This amends section 11's warning narration only. No rule, adoption, rollback,
retention, visibility or protocol admission semantics change.

Compare the actual judged rules for each effect, including omitted/default
rules. For updates use the engine's update_rule_judged: unnamed mutable-field
rules judge old and next; immutable/id-only inputs use its single-image
refinement. Named transitions judge their explicit images
(LLP 3000.007 section 8). Canonical Field/NextField terms load the same next
image; OldField stays distinct. The update-transition flag is represented by
this effective rule. Render the authored old and new clauses side by side. Propositional implication handles
allow/public/deny, conjunction, disjunction, negation, symmetric equality
and its inequality complement. Reference equality and probe atoms preserve the runtime Unknown verdict.
Negation/conjunction/disjunction use the same three-valued truth tables and
implication compares Readable admission only. Scalar equality is total; other
predicates remain independent atoms;
no ordering, database population, constraints or role-inhabitation proof is
invented. At most twelve distinct atoms and 4096 mixed Boolean/three-valued valuations
are enumerated; either limit leaves implication unproved.
Equivalent formulas get a note. A target that implies the old clause and
whose reverse implication fails in this abstraction gets a tightening note;
this proves no broader admission, not an inhabited strict difference.
Loosening and unproved implication keep WARNING. Rollback uses exactly the
same directional comparison, so selecting an old wider rule warns.

Suppress a rule warning only when its table's other effects and non-rule definition and all
other existing table definitions are unchanged, along with auth, maintained
views, timelines, delivery, receipts and receipt dependencies. Probe/reference atoms otherwise
may have different meanings. Self-table probes/references cannot claim stable
meaning while that table rule is changing and keep the warning. Authored mutation requirements are previewed with
paths and old/new predicates, refusal codes and messages, including nested
if/for/as requirements. Only a leading unconditional requirement prefix over
unchanged arguments, viewer, now and literals receives an implication proof;
Program equality on decoded scalar arguments is total, including null; it
does not use the reference/probe Unknown verdict. A nullable-argument live
regression checks that boundary. Locals, reads, functions, nested control flow and later requirements remain
unproved. Changed argument types or identity populations remain unproved. Equivalent
leading predicates with pairwise-equivalent predicates at each ordered
refusal code/message get a note; conjunction equivalence alone cannot prove
first-failure behavior. Unproved guards whose program/dependencies changed
retain a warning even if their printed clause is identical. Lexical bindings
are printed beside dependent guards. Equivalent predicates with
changed refusal codes/messages remain WARNING. Missing saved source never
fabricates an old requirement. Removed operations retain their own warning.

Acceptance covers widening, tightening, adding a requirement, SIGTERM,
rollback and forward against one durable deployment, paired with guide text.
Additional controls cover reordering, complementary equality, unknown changes,
the atom bound and schema dependency changes. REL3 owns dormant-visibility
counts; SURF owns clause comparison narration. Operator warnings are facts
about the preview snapshot, never an authorization decision.

Exact briefs, verbatim received bodies and Muse quota/fallback attempts are

Material findings were repaired and retained as live regressions: Unknown

## 13. Wave 13 preview decisions

PREV, implemented by Codex on 2026-10-16, amends §12.2 and REL3 §4.
Only narration changes; admission, retention, adoption and sync remain as before.

A guard is silent when its authored clause/refusal/context and argument types
are unchanged and its consulted schema inputs are unchanged. Dependency
comparison follows the compiled guard's reads, lexical inputs and enclosing
control flow, including branch joins, list accumulation, expanded function
bodies and earlier writes to consulted populations. Unrelated writes and unused
arguments are excluded. Maintained parent fields lead to their private maintain
and source population, in both guard comparison and user-effect narration;
whole-schema inequality is never the comparison. Point existence
uses its selected key/index columns, read rule and transitive probes/reference
hops, auth, and population visibility (through, opening, expiry, derivations).
Field reads also consult their column type, default and field authority.
A changed unused table or operation, or an additive unconsulted probe column,
is not evidence of a guard change. Existing value-domain changes and column
removals on a consulted population remain dependencies: they may hide or restore
rows, including through the reference-closed population and delete policies.
Loop-carried assignments/effects are included beyond the first iteration.
Consulted timeline, delivery and receipt metadata/field closures are compared
per population; unrelated metadata is excluded. Optional defaults read
as null under the existing interpreter and are not reported as literal fills. Changed dependencies with identical clauses are grouped by
changed dependency set, counting guards and listing affected operations once;
implication remains unproved and the operator must verify refusal cases.
Leading unconditional `exists` on scalar args/viewer/now/literals is a Boolean
atom only under unchanged dependencies. Unknown reference rules, nested/later
guards and first-failure refusal associations retain §12.2's conservative cut.
Both clauses' field dependencies are compared under both schemas before a rule
implication is certified. Tightening a requirement is a note only if each old
first-failure region still reaches the same refusal code/message; changed
refusal contracts and unproved associations remain warnings. Earlier effects
include their own read/control dependencies transitively, and argument-list
loop domains retain the used argument's type. First-failure proofs use the same
supported formula fragment: reference/unsupported/unstable-probe uncertainty
stays Unknown; only stable program existence is a Boolean atom. Narration never
grants admission.

A nullable reference added/removed outside a guard's actual reads/keys is not a
population dependency when the private preview snapshot proves every active and
retained image has that field absent/null and its default is null/absent. This
proof decodes storage values, includes whole-row and cell retention plus captured
reference identities, and fails closed on non-null data or invalid storage. It
filters only implicit population/domain/delete-edge dependencies; explicit
column, key and rule dependencies remain. Populated and restorable reference
images retain their parent closure. The proof is snapshot-bound, not a promise
about future writes.

Exact previews remain privileged operator output under §3/§9.1; row ids,
samples and values are never added to application responses. Exact previews
lead with user effects, in this order: query/count read dependencies
whose table active population shrinks (including point reads, lists and reads
of maintained values, not just aggregate queries); reference dependents lost with parents;
then mutation/job and written-rule safety dependencies that cannot see dormant
rows. Each category groups by table and lists operations once. Population counts
are table counts, not invented per-viewer query results, predicate matches or
business denominators. Point/list/asset-producing queries and maintained reads
share that first category; caps remain real adoption refusals/steps, and SQL
inspection shares the selected active table populations under §9.1. No arbitrary
query or SQL execution is claimed. Dependent counts use actual lost row/reference identities
from the private before/after snapshot. Existing dormant inventory, samples and
PUBFIX28's disjoint row/cell/index ownership stay authoritative. Earlier dormant
storage is not reported as newly lost active rows. Warnings ask the operator to
accept changed results/denominators, preserve dependents, or preserve a gate in a
bridge/pause the workflow. Schema steps and other counted notes follow effects.

Deploy receipts and previews report literal-default backfills as
`table.column ← value on N rows`: active candidate rows that lack the physical
field and gain its interpreted literal value on this switch. This is schema
interpretation, not a claim of physical writes. A fitting retained row/cell
image restored before interpretation is an explicit candidate value and is not
a backfill. Whole-table and standalone-cell restoration precede this count;
only fields still physically omitted in active candidate rows qualify. Existing explicit values,
standalone dormant cells and dormant rows do not count. Receipt facts are
computed inside successful activation, including hot activation; previews use
the same report. Changing a default can affect legacy absent fields and is
reported too. A type change alone does not imply a fill: optional-null rows
retained dormant by narrowing are excluded. Unchanged absence interpretation
and fresh empty tables do not invent work.

The deploy guide puts a concrete bridge sequence beside enum widening: preserve
value domain, audience and safety checks, migrate/repair, prove refusal, then
contract. H9's additive probe case has zero WARNINGs and fails on 0.4.29.
Golden previews cover all three user-effect categories, plus grouped changed
probe dependencies and populated backfills. PREV's report records five copied
farm lanes before/after. DAY3 owns saved-source/client-baseline composition;
KIT2 owns proof helper and frontend recipes; SURF3 owns general CLI/help prose.

## 14. Wave 14 rollback narration

PREV2, implemented by Codex on 2026-10-16, amends §§12.2 and 13. This is
operator narration only. Admission, retention, adoption, rule evaluation and
sync semantics do not change.

A probe population no longer imports every column domain of its table. Its
dependencies include the selected key/index columns, filter/equality columns,
projected or otherwise read fields, the read rule, auth and the transitive
visibility/population metadata already named in §13. A scan or point-presence
test does not by itself consult every returned row field: identity, presence and
cardinality consult population/key metadata, while scalar field access and
filters consult the named field domains; a whole-row value comparison consults
all of that row's fields. Aggregate value projections and
ordering/group keys likewise consult their explicitly named field metadata.
Forward widening of a consulted domain is monotone for the existing guard and
is not dependency uncertainty only when absent stored cells keep the same
interpretation; required-to-optional changes remain dependencies because a
literal default can become null. Selecting
the narrower release keeps §11's exact stored-value warning and §13's snapshot
population/safety warning; these count actual incompatible images and do not
recreate a table-wide guard warning. Defaults, field authority, maintained
inputs, removed consulted fields and non-widening domain changes remain
dependencies.

Leading unconditional requirements compare as their ordered conjunction. Thus
`require A else X` becoming `require A else X; require B else Y` is a proved
tightening when A's first-failure region still returns the same code/message
and both predicates' inputs are supported and stable. Inserting, reordering or
changing an old refusal contract remains unproved. Displayed table rules and
requirements preserve Boolean precedence with parentheses, independently of
the implication proof.

Rollback resolves and prints its selected immutable release before preview or
live activation; JSON keeps stdout machine-readable and sends that line to
stderr. The exact private-snapshot preview then says what users of the selected
release get back. A proved loosening identifies a lost current safety rule or
mutation guard, while a read-rule loosening identifies lost current
confidentiality. Query/read dependencies whose active table population changes
show before and after table counts, and changed cap constraints show current
and target quotas. These remain table-population and declared-cap facts, never
invented per-viewer results or predicate matches. Each warning recommends
preserving the current domain, audience, guard or quota in a bridge release (or
pausing the workflow) before activation. Existing dormant row/cell/index
inventory and exact incompatible-value warnings remain authoritative.

Acceptance covers an unread enum widening with zero grouped guard warnings,
the populated rollback warning for that same widened value, an added trailing
requirement with preserved and changed refusal contracts, Boolean grouping,
rollback loss of safety and confidentiality, changed population and quota
narration with bridge advice, and resolved-target ordering before preview and
activation.

DAY4 final delta review: [Muse READY](reviews/3000.000.000-day4-r5.muse.md) and [fresh Codex READY](reviews/3000.000.000-day4-r5.codex.md), both on the same immutable revision; earlier rounds and exact source/transcript provenance are preserved alongside those records.

## 15. Wave 15 preview and rollback (DAY5)

Implementer: Codex for Charlie Cheever, 2026-10-18, target 0.4.32. This amends
§§12–14's operator narration. No admission, authorization, adoption, retention,
sync or expiry behavior changes.

An added pure trailing requirement after an identical read/binding/require
prefix proves tightening even when it reads a newly added column. Compare the
preserved prefix's compiled guards, argument inputs, lexical bindings and
transitive schema dependencies using the existing dependency analysis; exclude
only the newly appended requirement sites. The extra requirement admits a
subset of executions and preserves every old first-failure code/message. This
proof needs no old value for the new input. It supports literal/name/member,
unary and binary predicates; functions, effectful predicates, nested guards,
reordered requirements and changed old refusal contracts stay outside the proof.
Every authored requirement must be in that leading prefix. The reversed same
proof identifies removed trailing guards as a loosening on rollback. Independent
compiler-inserted guards retain their dependency evidence.

Human previews lead with lost guards and their refusal codes and group facts by
affected table or operation, keeping full qualifications and structural detail
under --verbose and in unchanged JSON evidence arrays. Related population,
domain, query and safety-dependency facts share one line. Index inventory stays
counted but internal index paths and hashes appear only with --verbose or JSON.
Receipts aggregate index samples by table. Privileged constraint refusals name
both restoring dormant row ids and conflicting active row ids from actual
constraint groups, bounded to five samples per category. The existing atomic
refusal and repair-through-a-bridge policy is unchanged.

Rollback previews expose an app-specific checklist from their actual hazards.
`rollback <deployment> --to N --check --bridge <new-dir>` writes a bridge starter
containing the complete currently deployed source and ROLLBACK-CHECKLIST.md.
It starts with all current safety/visibility behavior intact; the operator edits
it toward the target, repairs data through authorized mutations and proves the
listed refusal/query cases. This is a starting point, not a synthesized business
migration or a claim that the target is safe. The destination must be new;
existing source is never overwritten, and no bridge is activated automatically.

Validation: CLI regressions cover a row-bound trailing guard on a new column,
its backward removed refusal, changed refusal controls, bridge source identity,
read-only live state, immutable bridge paths, grouped rows and hidden index
paths. Existing population/refusal/retention suites remain authoritative.

Round-one review dispositions: Muse READY; fresh Codex NOT READY. Bridge
outputs now reject canonical deployment descendants, including symlink aliases.
Grouping retains complete safety/visibility/quota facts and every non-index
source change. Constraint evidence associates each restoring/active sample with
its actual duplicate key, bounded to five groups and five ids per category.
The complete verbatim reviews and initial source-hash provenance are retained;
source fixes during the first review are recorded as a moving-tree limitation,
not matching final-revision readiness. Another fresh mutually blind round is
required on the corrected source.

# LLP 3000.000.000.001: Staging with real users

**Type:** RFC
**Status:** Implemented
**Systems:** Snapback 4 deployment, password identity, development doors, proof kit
**Author:** Codex for Charlie Cheever (STAGE)
**Date:** 2026-10-18
**Revised:** 2026-10-18
**Related:** [Release activation](./3000.000.000-retained-storage-and-hot-release.rfc.md), [Proof kit](./3000.011.003.000.000-packaged-proof-kit.rfc.md), [Store ownership](./3000.019-what-the-builder-still-wrote.rfc.md#34-p8-residency-you-can-see-and-a-cli-that-does-not-fight-the-dev-server)

## 1. Commission and boundary

Implementer: STAGE, 2026-10-18, commissioned by Charlie's wave-16 bakeoff
fixes #1/#4. Ten Muse probes used dev because password-role provisioning
had no single recipe. This amendment supplies that recipe using the existing
signup and service-seat authority. No system seed door is added to serve.
SEC owns self-promotion lints and security invariants; KIT4 owns unrelated
proof helpers, PROD2 owns production starter adaptation.

`deploy <dir> --staging` classifies a **new deployment store**, under its
exclusive owner, with durable metadata `deployment_environment=staging`.
An existing store with no classification is production, including legacy stores;
it cannot be relabelled. A staging store remains staging through deploy,
rollback, restart, copying and source edits. There is no promote/relabel flag:
production requires a separate fresh deployment without --staging and real
operator provisioning. --check never creates the classification. Repeating
--staging on an already staging store is allowed, including while serve runs;
it verifies the existing classification without changing it. Malicious edits by
the filesystem/database owner are outside the threat model, as for service tokens.
This is a deployment-lifetime boundary stronger than a mutable per-release flag:
production source or a seed plan cannot opt a production store into test setup.

## 2. Real staging accounts

`GET /auth/config` reports `development` independently of door availability,
`devDoors`, and `environment` (`development`, `staging`, or `production`).
Missing/unknown environment metadata is production. serve remains bearer-only
in both environments, with the same password verifiers, session lifetimes,
rules, rate limits, clock and data plane. No password hashing or grant bypass.

`startServe(dir, options)` additionally returns `operatorToken(service =
"operator", days = 1)` and `seedAccounts(options)`. Minting uses the existing
local CLI proof protocol, captures secret stdout in memory and never writes
tokens into proof logs. It confers only that service seat's existing authority.
Tokens may also be requested on production for first-admin setup.

`seedAccounts({roles, provision, args?})` creates N real password accounts per
role, then calls the declared mutation as service:operator; `args(account)`
maps the account to application arguments (default `{principal, role}`).
`provision` is a generated mutation handle or `{name}`, including qualified names such as `accounts.provision`. The returned records
contain email, password, principal, token and expiresAt, grouped by role.
`startServe(dir, {seedUsers: options})` performs the same setup before returning
and exposes those records as `sessions`. Before minting or creating accounts,
the helper requires environment=staging and development=false. Production,
legacy and dev hosts refuse. Inputs are validated before effects; signup is
sequential, bounded, respects identity throttling, and never retries an ambiguous
write. A fresh call creates a fresh random email/password set. Partial failure
does not claim rollback: some accounts/grants may exist; discard the disposable
staging deployment or inspect it before retrying. Application data is created
through ordinary authenticated mutations after provisioning.

Credential records are returned to the operator, never logged by the helper.
The tested guide writes them to an exclusively created 0600 file. They must
not enter source control or reports. There is no public provisioning HTTP
endpoint beyond the application's declared rule-checked mutation.

## 3. First admin

The tested guide uses password signup (which grants no role), then the
filesystem owner's `operatorToken()` to invoke an operator-only mutation.
The roster's insert rule independently requires service:operator. No "first
caller wins", empty-roster bypass, client-selected signup role, or permanent
operator credential in the frontend. The same recipe works on production
with explicit operator-selected email/password; bulk seedAccounts refuses there.
Privileged account tokens cannot mint service tokens. SEC owns lints for
self-bootstrapped global authority; the guide points to this ownership.

## 4. Development doors

For password-only schemas (`auth.password && !auth.guests`), dev disables
`/seed`, every `/dev/*` door (except the existing cryptographically protected
local token protocol), and persona headers by default. The pre-existing serve
GET /dev/latency inspection remains service-authenticated; it never admits an
unauthenticated caller and its reset remains refused. `dev
--unsafe-dev-doors` is explicit opt-in with a prominent warning; serve accepts
no such flag. Host/Origin loopback fences still apply. Door availability is
computed from the current adopted schema, so hot adoption to password-only
also closes them. Seed/reset, clock advance and dev job enqueue revalidate
against the engine schema under the writer immediately before privileged effects;
slow request bodies and requests queued behind adoption cannot retain authority.
Action admission/replay, session-route admission, persona cancellation and upload
owner state transitions likewise revalidate under their actual writer. Previously
admitted durable work retains the existing work-record contract; a refused
new request creates no claimable work.
Persona reads also revalidate against the engine used to evaluate the query,
sync page or asset grant, checking the current declared cast at that read’s
admission. In-flight reads retain their existing snapshot semantics: cast
publication does not lock out concurrent ordinary writes, and the engine
transaction rejects a replaced execution schema.
Change-poll presence entry revalidates under its engine. A detached old
schema is fenced by the engine transaction; a replacement password-only reader
cannot inherit a pre-adoption persona capability. A persona header must name an explicitly declared persona;
an empty declaration admits none. Persona-based schemas retain their ordinary
development workflow. /auth/config always reports development=true on dev,
even with closed doors, so probe tooling can refuse it.

`startDev` follows native persistent storage by default, consuming the old
--no-memory alias; --memory/--fresh explicitly request disposable storage.
The returned now() refuses with guidance if the clock door is closed. Native
readiness plus the per-launch proof marker still establish host identity when
/dev/status is unavailable. Existing fixture/clock tests explicitly opt into
unsafe dev doors when their password schema requires those tools. Generated
frontend proofs explicitly select --memory and --unsafe-dev-doors, preserving
disposable fixture isolation and repeatability under the persistent helper
default. Exact 0.4.30/0.4.31 proof templates are retained and safely adapted
by digest; custom scripts remain untouched. DAY5 owns other starter custody.

self-grant and unauthenticated doors refused; default/explicit dev doors and

logs; the exact guide recipe executed from the packed tarball in Linux
